Like the kind of voices from work, over the system, calling my operator number. Not vague, not like I’m imagining a memory of it. It sounds like it’s actually coming through, like someone keyed up and said it.\n\nThey’ll say my number, and then things like I’m dangerous, or that security is coming for me. And I’ll sit there trying to figure out where it’s coming from, because I know I’m not at the warehouse, I know there shouldn’t be any dispatch in my garage. I’ve checked around, I’ve turned things off, I’ve listened to see if it’s neighbors or some device, but it’s still… it’s right there, like it’s being directed at me.\n\nIt scares me, honestly. Because when you hear your operator number in your own garage, with everything quiet, it doesn’t feel like just stress. It feels like they can still reach me.\n\nDoctor: Outside of work and away from the scanners or the computer system, do you ever have thoughts or beliefs that others don’t share—like feeling followed, being part of a special mission, or that something unusual is happening that you can’t explain?\n\nPatient: Yeah… mostly it all ties back to work, but it doesn’t stay neatly at work in my head, if that makes sense.\n\nI keep coming back to the routing system, because that’s where it started making itself known. It isn’t just a computer to me anymore. I know people hear me say that and they think I’m making too much out of warehouse software, but I’ve worked around those systems long enough to know normal glitches from something else. This has been marking my moves—where I go, how long I take, which aisle I’m sent to, what scanner prompt comes up next. It sends messages through the scanners, not like plain English all the time, but through the pattern of it, the timing, the way it boxes me in.\n\nAnd the reason I think it’s happening is because I’m one of the old hands there. I’ve been around long enough to know how the place runs, and I think they want me pushed out without saying it directly. So instead of somebody coming up and telling me, they use the system to make me look bad—slow me down, make my routes impossible, make it seem like I’m the problem.\n\nSo, no, I wouldn’t say it’s a “special mission” exactly. It’s more like something unusual is happening and other people either don’t see it or don’t want to admit it. But I can tell when the system is marking me. It’s not random.\n\nDoctor: Do you believe, even when you’re away from work and not hearing the voices, that the routing system is still actively working against you or plotting something against you?\n\nPatient: Yes. Even when I’m away from work, even when it’s quiet and nothing is happening right in front of me, I still believe it’s working against me.\n\nThat’s part of why I’m here, honestly. The routing system at the warehouse isn’t just a computer to me anymore. I know how that sounds, but I’ve been there a long time, and I know the difference between a bad day, bad software, and something that’s marking your moves. It’s like it tracks what I do, how I respond, where I go, and then it uses that against me the next time.\n\nAnd the scanners are part of it too. The way the prompts come through, the timing, the routes it gives me—it feels like messages. Not always words, but messages through the pattern. Like it’s telling me, “We’ve got you,” or “You’re not wanted here,” and then it sets me up to look slow or confused or like I can’t do the job anymore.\n\nI think it’s because I’m one of the old hands. I’ve been around long enough to know how things used to run, and I think that makes me a problem to them. So instead of somebody saying it directly, the system gets used to push me out. Quietly. Make me fail enough times that they can point to numbers and say, “See, he can’t keep up.”\n\nSo yes, even away from work, I don’t stop believing it. I may try not to think about it, but it’s still there in my mind. It’s still active. It’s still aimed at me.\n\nDoctor: Can you think of a time when something happened that, to you, proved beyond a doubt that the routing system was working against you—even though others might have explained it differently or not seen it the same way?\n\nPatient: Yes. The one that sticks with me is when the routing system started sending me in these circles—like not just a bad route, not just inefficient, but the kind of pattern where it felt like it was watching how I reacted and then adjusting to make me look worse.\n\nI’d get a prompt on the scanner, go where it told me, and then right after that it would send me clear across the floor, then back near where I had just been. And I know people can say, “Well, that’s just how the system batches orders,” or “Maybe it was busy,” or whatever. I’ve heard those explanations. But I’ve been doing this long enough to know the normal nonsense from the other kind. This had a rhythm to it. It was marking my moves.\n\nIt wasn’t only where it sent me, it was the timing of the prompts. Like I’d hesitate for half a second, or I’d try to do it the efficient way, and then the next message on the scanner would come through like it was answering me. Not in a regular sentence, but the way it boxed me in—like, “No, we saw that. Go here now. Let’s see you keep up.” And then if I looked slow, that would be on me, right? My numbers would show I couldn’t keep pace.\n\nThat’s what proved it to me. Because it wasn’t random. It was too directed. It was using the scanner and the routes to push me out because I’m one of the old hands there, and I know how the place is supposed to run. People who haven’t been there as long might not see it. They’d just say the computer picked the route. But I could tell it was aimed at me.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had similar beliefs or experiences outside of work—like feeling that technology or systems are targeting you in other areas of your life?\n\nPatient: Not in the same exact way, no… I mean, I notice things outside of work sometimes, but the thing that really has teeth in it is the warehouse system.\n\nThat’s why I keep coming back to it, because it isn’t just “technology is bad” or me thinking every machine is after me. It’s that routing system specifically. I’m here because that system isn’t just a computer to me anymore. It’s been marking my moves for a long time now—where I go, how fast I’m moving, when I hesitate, what aisle I’m in—and then the scanners send things through in a way that feels like messages. The timing, the routes, the way it puts me in a corner… that’s not random to me.\n\nAnd I think it’s aimed at me because I’m one of the old hands there. I’ve been around long enough to know when something is just a normal mistake versus when something is being used to push somebody out. Other people might say it’s just software, but I don’t experience it like that. It feels directed. It feels like it’s trying to make me look like I can’t keep up anymore.\n\nSo outside of work, I wouldn’t say I’ve got the same proof with, like, every other system. But it follows me in my head because I know when I go back in, it’s still there. The warehouse system is the main one. That’s the one that’s targeting me.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had thoughts or beliefs that most people would find extremely strange or not based in reality—like believing something has control over your thoughts or that you’re receiving special messages that no one else can understand?\n\nPatient: Yes… I mean, I know most people would probably say it sounds strange. I’m aware of that. If I said it in the break room, they’d look at me like I’d lost it, so I don’t go around saying it much.\n\nBut to me, the routing system at the warehouse isn’t just a computer anymore. That’s the thing. It feels like it knows my movements. It marks what I do—where I go, when I hesitate, how fast I’m moving, whether I’m trying to work around the route—and then it sends things back through the scanners in a way that feels like messages. Not like a voice in my head exactly, but the prompts, the timing, the route changes… they mean something.\n\nAnd I think it’s doing it to push me out. I’ve been there a long time. I’m one of the old hands, and I know how things used to run. I think that makes me someone they want gone, or at least someone the system is being used against. It sets me up to look slow, confused, like I can’t keep up anymore.\n\nSo yes, if you’re asking whether I believe I’m getting messages other people don’t understand, I’d say yes—through the scanners and the routing patterns. Other people may just see a screen telling them where to go. I see something aimed at me.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever have thoughts or beliefs that are completely separate from your feelings—like believing something is happening or being communicated to you even when you're not upset, anxious, or in a bad mood?\n\nPatient: Yes… yes, that’s happened. That’s actually part of what makes it hard for me to just write it off as me being in a bad mood or being overwhelmed.\n\nThere was a stretch where I wasn’t as down as I had been. I was eating a little better, talking a little more—nothing perfect, but I wasn’t in that real low place every minute. People around me probably would’ve said I seemed a bit more like myself. But even then, for weeks, I still had this thing going on where I’d hear dispatch calling my number, like it was coming through the way it does at work, and I still believed the routing system was tracking me.\n\nAnd that’s what bothers me, because it wasn’t only when I was upset or worked up. I could be calmer, just sitting there or getting through the day, and it would still be there in my mind—the feeling that the system had my number, that it knew where I was, that it was following what I did. So, yes, it can be separate from my mood. The mood may make it worse, maybe, but the belief didn’t just go away when I was feeling a little better.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever believed something that you know most people would never accept as true—something that might seem completely impossible or strange, even to people who know you well?\n\nPatient: Yes. I know most people wouldn’t accept it. Even people who know me—if I sat them down and really explained it, they’d probably say, “That’s not possible, that’s just a warehouse computer, that’s just routing software.” I understand how it sounds when I say it out loud.\n\nBut I’m here because, to me, that routing system at the warehouse isn’t just a computer anymore. It’s been marking my moves. I mean, not just generally tracking work like systems do—I mean it feels like it knows me. Where I go, when I slow down, when I hesitate, when I try to avoid a bad aisle or take a different path, it’s like it catches that and then answers back through the scanners.\n\nThe scanner prompts, the routes it gives me, the timing of when certain jobs come through… it feels like messages. Not messages everybody can read, maybe, but messages aimed at me. And I believe it’s doing that to push me out because I’m one of the old hands there. I’ve been there long enough to know the place, know the patterns, know when something is just normal confusion and when something feels arranged.\n\nSo yes, I know that sounds strange. I know someone else would say, “That can’t be true.” But for me it doesn’t feel like a theory. It feels like something I’ve been living with.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever believed something that you yourself recognized as completely impossible or not real, even though it still felt true to you at the time?\n\nPatient: Yes… and that’s the part that makes me feel kind of split in two when I talk about it.\n\nBecause there’s one part of me that can hear how impossible it sounds. I know a warehouse routing system is supposed to be just software—numbers, locations, productivity, scanner prompts, all that. I know if someone else came to me and said, “The system is watching me personally and sending messages through the scanner,” I’d probably think they were under a lot of stress or reading too much into things.\n\nBut when I’m in it—when I’m there, and the scanner changes the route right after I hesitate, or it sends me back through the same bad section, or it gives me something that feels timed exactly to what I just did—it doesn’t feel fake. It feels aimed at me. It feels like it’s marking my moves, like it knows I’m one of the old hands and it’s trying to make me look slow or confused so I’ll be pushed out.\n\nSo yes, I guess I have recognized that it sounds completely impossible… but at the same time, it still felt true. Not like I was pretending to believe it. It felt real to me, even while another part of me knew most people would say it couldn’t be.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever believed something so strange or impossible that even you had trouble understanding how it could be true—like something completely out of the ordinary, not just other people disagreeing with you?\n\nPatient: Yes… yes, I think that’s exactly what it is, or close to it. Because even I have trouble explaining how it could be true in a normal way.\n\nI mean, I know what the routing system is supposed to be. I worked around that kind of thing long enough to know it’s supposed to assign routes and track work and send things through the scanner. It’s not supposed to have intentions. It’s not supposed to know a person the way a person knows you. So when I try to explain it, I get stuck, because I can hear myself and I think, How would that even work?\n\nBut I’m here because the routing system at the warehouse isn’t just a computer to me anymore. I know it’s been marking my moves. Not just recording them like any work system would, but marking them, noticing them—when I slow down, when I avoid a certain path, when I try to get ahead of it. And then the scanner sends things back at me in a way that feels like messages. Like it’s answering what I just did.\n\nAnd the part that feels almost impossible is that I believe it’s doing it to push me out because I’m one of the old hands there. Like I’ve been there too long, I know too much of how the place works, and the system—or whatever is behind it—is trying to make me look bad. I know that sounds out of the ordinary. I know it’s not just, “Oh, my boss doesn’t like me,” or “The schedule is unfair.” It feels stranger than that. It feels like the system itself has singled me out.\n\nSo yes, even I have trouble understanding how it could be true. But when it’s happening, it doesn’t feel like a misunderstanding. It feels real.\n\nDoctor: Can you think of any time when someone showed you clear proof that the routing system wasn’t targeting you, and if so, did that change your belief at all?\n\nPatient: There have been times people tried to show me it wasn’t personal, yes. Like they’d say, “Look, everybody gets rerouted,” or they’d point out that the scanner sends odd jobs to other people too, or that the system is just balancing the workload. And I can sit there and nod, because on paper that sounds reasonable. I’m not saying I can’t understand the explanation.\n\nBut it didn’t really change what I believed. Not in the way they wanted it to.\n\nBecause when I’m actually on the floor and it happens to me, it’s different. It’s the timing of it, the way it seems to catch me right when I hesitate or try to avoid a section, the way the scanner sends me somewhere that feels like it’s answering what I just did. That’s the part other people don’t see. They see a routing screen or a list of jobs and say, “See, it’s random,” but I’m the one walking it, I’m the one feeling how it lines up.\n\nSo even if someone showed me something that looked like proof, I’d still think, well, that only proves what it shows on the surface. It doesn’t prove it isn’t marking my moves underneath that. It doesn’t prove the messages aren’t meant for me. And I know how that sounds, I do, but the belief doesn’t just go away because someone explains the software. It still feels like the system is trying to push me out because I’ve been there so long and I know the place too well.\n\nDoctor: Has anyone ever shown you clear evidence that the routing system wasn't targeting you, and if so, did that change your belief at all?\n\nPatient: Yes… people have tried. They’ve shown me screens, or explained how the routing is supposed to work, like it’s just balancing tasks or moving people around based on workload. They’ll say, “See, it’s not you. Everyone gets these scanner changes.” And I understand what they’re trying to prove. I really do.\n\nBut it doesn’t settle it for me, because the evidence they show is always… surface evidence, if that makes sense. It shows what the computer says it’s doing. It doesn’t show why it happens exactly when it happens to me. It doesn’t explain why the scanner sends me back through certain places right after I hesitate, or why it feels like it’s responding to my movements, like it’s marking what I do.\n\nI know it sounds unreasonable when I say it out loud, but I’m here because the routing system at the warehouse isn’t just a computer to me anymore. I know it’s been marking my moves and sending messages through the scanners, and it feels like it’s doing that to push me out because I’m one of the old hands there. I’ve been around long enough, I know how the place works, and it feels like the system knows that too.\n\nSo no, the proof hasn’t really changed the belief. Maybe for a minute I can nod along and think, okay, maybe that explains part of it. But once I’m back there and the scanner starts doing it again, it all comes right back. It feels too targeted to be nothing.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Give candidates as ICD-10 codes, not disease names.\n- If the list changed from the previous candidates, say in \"note\" what this turn added or ruled out. Where I go, when I slow down, when I hesitate, when I try to avoid a bad aisle or take a different path, it’s like it catches that and then answers back through the scanners.\n\nThe scanner prompts, the routes it gives me, the timing of when certain jobs come through… it feels like messages. Not messages everybody can read, maybe, but messages aimed at me. And I believe it’s doing that to push me out because I’m one of the old hands there. I’ve been there long enough to know the place, know the patterns, know when something is just normal confusion and when something feels arranged.\n\nSo yes, I know that sounds strange. I know someone else would say, “That can’t be true.” But for me it doesn’t feel like a theory. It feels like something I’ve been living with.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever believed something that you yourself recognized as completely impossible or not real, even though it still felt true to you at the time?\n\nPatient: Yes… and that’s the part that makes me feel kind of split in two when I talk about it.\n\nBecause there’s one part of me that can hear how impossible it sounds. I know a warehouse routing system is supposed to be just software—numbers, locations, productivity, scanner prompts, all that. I know if someone else came to me and said, “The system is watching me personally and sending messages through the scanner,” I’d probably think they were under a lot of stress or reading too much into things.\n\nBut when I’m in it—when I’m there, and the scanner changes the route right after I hesitate, or it sends me back through the same bad section, or it gives me something that feels timed exactly to what I just did—it doesn’t feel fake. It feels aimed at me. It feels like it’s marking my moves, like it knows I’m one of the old hands and it’s trying to make me look slow or confused so I’ll be pushed out.\n\nSo yes, I guess I have recognized that it sounds completely impossible… but at the same time, it still felt true. Not like I was pretending to believe it. It felt real to me, even while another part of me knew most people would say it couldn’t be.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever believed something so strange or impossible that even you had trouble understanding how it could be true—like something completely out of the ordinary, not just other people disagreeing with you?\n\nPatient: Yes… yes, I think that’s exactly what it is, or close to it. Because even I have trouble explaining how it could be true in a normal way.\n\nI mean, I know what the routing system is supposed to be. I worked around that kind of thing long enough to know it’s supposed to assign routes and track work and send things through the scanner. It’s not supposed to have intentions. It’s not supposed to know a person the way a person knows you. So when I try to explain it, I get stuck, because I can hear myself and I think, How would that even work?\n\nBut I’m here because the routing system at the warehouse isn’t just a computer to me anymore. I know it’s been marking my moves. Not just recording them like any work system would, but marking them, noticing them—when I slow down, when I avoid a certain path, when I try to get ahead of it. And then the scanner sends things back at me in a way that feels like messages. Like it’s answering what I just did.\n\nAnd the part that feels almost impossible is that I believe it’s doing it to push me out because I’m one of the old hands there. Like I’ve been there too long, I know too much of how the place works, and the system—or whatever is behind it—is trying to make me look bad. I know that sounds out of the ordinary. I know it’s not just, “Oh, my boss doesn’t like me,” or “The schedule is unfair.” It feels stranger than that. It feels like the system itself has singled me out.\n\nSo yes, even I have trouble understanding how it could be true. But when it’s happening, it doesn’t feel like a misunderstanding. It feels real.\n\nDoctor: Can you think of any time when someone showed you clear proof that the routing system wasn’t targeting you, and if so, did that change your belief at all?\n\nPatient: There have been times people tried to show me it wasn’t personal, yes. Like they’d say, “Look, everybody gets rerouted,” or they’d point out that the scanner sends odd jobs to other people too, or that the system is just balancing the workload. And I can sit there and nod, because on paper that sounds reasonable. I’m not saying I can’t understand the explanation.\n\nBut it didn’t really change what I believed. Not in the way they wanted it to.\n\nBecause when I’m actually on the floor and it happens to me, it’s different. It’s the timing of it, the way it seems to catch me right when I hesitate or try to avoid a section, the way the scanner sends me somewhere that feels like it’s answering what I just did. That’s the part other people don’t see. They see a routing screen or a list of jobs and say, “See, it’s random,” but I’m the one walking it, I’m the one feeling how it lines up.\n\nSo even if someone showed me something that looked like proof, I’d still think, well, that only proves what it shows on the surface. It doesn’t prove it isn’t marking my moves underneath that. It doesn’t prove the messages aren’t meant for me. And I know how that sounds, I do, but the belief doesn’t just go away because someone explains the software. It still feels like the system is trying to push me out because I’ve been there so long and I know the place too well.\n\nDoctor: Has anyone ever shown you clear evidence that the routing system wasn't targeting you, and if so, did that change your belief at all?\n\nPatient: Yes… people have tried. They’ve shown me screens, or explained how the routing is supposed to work, like it’s just balancing tasks or moving people around based on workload. They’ll say, “See, it’s not you. Everyone gets these scanner changes.” And I understand what they’re trying to prove. I really do.\n\nBut it doesn’t settle it for me, because the evidence they show is always… surface evidence, if that makes sense. It shows what the computer says it’s doing. It doesn’t show why it happens exactly when it happens to me. It doesn’t explain why the scanner sends me back through certain places right after I hesitate, or why it feels like it’s responding to my movements, like it’s marking what I do.\n\nI know it sounds unreasonable when I say it out loud, but I’m here because the routing system at the warehouse isn’t just a computer to me anymore. I know it’s been marking my moves and sending messages through the scanners, and it feels like it’s doing that to push me out because I’m one of the old hands there. I’ve been around long enough, I know how the place works, and it feels like the system knows that too.\n\nSo no, the proof hasn’t really changed the belief. Maybe for a minute I can nod along and think, okay, maybe that explains part of it. But once I’m back there and the scanner starts doing it again, it all comes right back. It feels too targeted to be nothing.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Ask about exactly ONE thing. Never bundle several symptoms, or several attributes of one symptom, into a single question — e.g. asking whether a symptom is present and how long it has lasted at the same time.\n- If you need to ask about more than one thing, ask the one that tells you the most now and come back to the rest later.\n- Keep the question clear enough for the patient to understand.\n- Respond in English.\n\n[Output Format — JSON only, no other text]\n{\"intent\": \"<what you want to find out with this question>\", \"question\": \"<how you actually ask it in the interview>\"}\n"

========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Yes. Deep down, yes.

I can listen to the explanation, and I can even repeat it back—how the routing system assigns work, how the scanners update, how it’s supposed to be based on workload or whatever pattern the company set up. I’m not saying I don’t understand the official version. I do. I’ve been around equipment and systems long enough to know what people mean when they say, “It’s just the program.”

But that doesn’t get to what I’m talking about. Because I’m here because the routing system at the warehouse isn’t just a computer to me anymore. It feels like it’s watching my movements, marking them, like it knows when I’m trying to avoid a certain lane or when I slow down or when I’m trying to stay ahead of it. And then the scanner sends something back that feels like a message. Not words exactly, but a direction, a change, a job assignment that lands in a way that feels meant for me.

So even if someone shows me proof, there’s this part of me that thinks, That’s just what it shows on the outside. It doesn’t prove what’s underneath. It doesn’t prove it isn’t singling me out. And I know that sounds hard to accept, maybe even impossible, but it feels too exact when it happens.

I still believe it’s trying to push me out because I’m one of the old hands there. I’ve been there long enough, I know how things work, and it feels like the system knows that and wants me gone. So… yes. No matter what they show me, I can’t fully let go of that.

I operate industrial trucks over at the warehouse, been doing that kind of work a long time, and the routing system they use—the computer system that tells you where to go, what to pick up, where to drop things—it doesn’t feel like just a computer to me anymore.\n\nIt’s been marking my moves. I know that sounds like a lot, but it’s not just a feeling. The scanners, the little handheld ones and the ones on the trucks, they send these messages, like through the routes and the timing and what pops up. It’s like they’re steering me, watching how I respond, pushing me into mistakes or trying to make me look slow. And I think it’s because I’m one of the old hands there. I’ve been around, I know the place, and now it feels like the system is being used to push me out.\n\nI tried telling myself it was just glitches or changes in the software, but it keeps lining up too neatly. The routes, the scanner prompts, the way it sends me back and forth… it’s like it’s singling me out. That’s why I came in. I can’t keep acting like it’s nothing.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel that the computer system is intentionally trying to harm you or make you fail, even though others might not see it that way?\n\nPatient: Yes. I mean… yes, that’s what it feels like, and more than feels like. I know other people might say, “It’s just routing software,” or “Everybody gets bad routes sometimes,” but this isn’t random to me. It’s too patterned.\n\nThe system marks my moves. The scanners, the truck prompts, the way the routes come up—one after another, like it’s watching how I respond and then adjusting. It sends me in circles, gives me timing that makes me look behind, puts me where I’ll get jammed up, then acts like I’m the one making mistakes. And I think it’s because I’m one of the old hands there. I’ve been there long enough to know how things used to run, and now it’s like the computer is being used to push me out, quietly, so nobody has to come right out and say it.\n\nOther people may not see it because it’s not aimed at them the same way. But when you’re the one getting the messages through the scanners and the routes, you can tell. It’s intentional. It’s trying to make me fail.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever hear, see, or feel things that others don’t, like voices, images, or sensations, even when you’re not looking at a screen or scanner?\n\nPatient: Yes… I do. I wasn’t sure if I should say that part right away because it makes me sound worse, but yes.\n\nIt’s not only when I’m at work or looking at the scanner. Sometimes I’ll be home, sitting out in my garage, and everything is shut off—no radio, no TV, no phone making noise, nothing running—and I’ll hear dispatch voices clear as day. Like the kind of voices from work, over the system, calling my operator number. Not vague, not like I’m imagining a memory of it. It sounds like it’s actually coming through, like someone keyed up and said it.\n\nThey’ll say my number, and then things like I’m dangerous, or that security is coming for me. And I’ll sit there trying to figure out where it’s coming from, because I know I’m not at the warehouse, I know there shouldn’t be any dispatch in my garage. I’ve checked around, I’ve turned things off, I’ve listened to see if it’s neighbors or some device, but it’s still… it’s right there, like it’s being directed at me.\n\nIt scares me, honestly. Because when you hear your operator number in your own garage, with everything quiet, it doesn’t feel like just stress. It feels like they can still reach me.\n\nDoctor: Outside of work and away from the scanners or the computer system, do you ever have thoughts or beliefs that others don’t share—like feeling followed, being part of a special mission, or that something unusual is happening that you can’t explain?\n\nPatient: Yeah… mostly it all ties back to work, but it doesn’t stay neatly at work in my head, if that makes sense.\n\nI keep coming back to the routing system, because that’s where it started making itself known. It isn’t just a computer to me anymore. I know people hear me say that and they think I’m making too much out of warehouse software, but I’ve worked around those systems long enough to know normal glitches from something else. This has been marking my moves—where I go, how long I take, which aisle I’m sent to, what scanner prompt comes up next. It sends messages through the scanners, not like plain English all the time, but through the pattern of it, the timing, the way it boxes me in.\n\nAnd the reason I think it’s happening is because I’m one of the old hands there. I’ve been around long enough to know how the place runs, and I think they want me pushed out without saying it directly. So instead of somebody coming up and telling me, they use the system to make me look bad—slow me down, make my routes impossible, make it seem like I’m the problem.\n\nSo, no, I wouldn’t say it’s a “special mission” exactly. It’s more like something unusual is happening and other people either don’t see it or don’t want to admit it. But I can tell when the system is marking me. It’s not random.\n\nDoctor: Do you believe, even when you’re away from work and not hearing the voices, that the routing system is still actively working against you or plotting something against you?\n\nPatient: Yes. Even when I’m away from work, even when it’s quiet and nothing is happening right in front of me, I still believe it’s working against me.\n\nThat’s part of why I’m here, honestly. The routing system at the warehouse isn’t just a computer to me anymore. I know how that sounds, but I’ve been there a long time, and I know the difference between a bad day, bad software, and something that’s marking your moves. It’s like it tracks what I do, how I respond, where I go, and then it uses that against me the next time.\n\nAnd the scanners are part of it too. The way the prompts come through, the timing, the routes it gives me—it feels like messages. Not always words, but messages through the pattern. Like it’s telling me, “We’ve got you,” or “You’re not wanted here,” and then it sets me up to look slow or confused or like I can’t do the job anymore.\n\nI think it’s because I’m one of the old hands. I’ve been around long enough to know how things used to run, and I think that makes me a problem to them. So instead of somebody saying it directly, the system gets used to push me out. Quietly. Make me fail enough times that they can point to numbers and say, “See, he can’t keep up.”\n\nSo yes, even away from work, I don’t stop believing it. I may try not to think about it, but it’s still there in my mind. It’s still active. It’s still aimed at me.\n\nDoctor: Can you think of a time when something happened that, to you, proved beyond a doubt that the routing system was working against you—even though others might have explained it differently or not seen it the same way?\n\nPatient: Yes. The one that sticks with me is when the routing system started sending me in these circles—like not just a bad route, not just inefficient, but the kind of pattern where it felt like it was watching how I reacted and then adjusting to make me look worse.\n\nI’d get a prompt on the scanner, go where it told me, and then right after that it would send me clear across the floor, then back near where I had just been. And I know people can say, “Well, that’s just how the system batches orders,” or “Maybe it was busy,” or whatever. I’ve heard those explanations. But I’ve been doing this long enough to know the normal nonsense from the other kind. This had a rhythm to it. It was marking my moves.\n\nIt wasn’t only where it sent me, it was the timing of the prompts. Like I’d hesitate for half a second, or I’d try to do it the efficient way, and then the next message on the scanner would come through like it was answering me. Not in a regular sentence, but the way it boxed me in—like, “No, we saw that. Go here now. Let’s see you keep up.” And then if I looked slow, that would be on me, right? My numbers would show I couldn’t keep pace.\n\nThat’s what proved it to me. Because it wasn’t random. It was too directed. It was using the scanner and the routes to push me out because I’m one of the old hands there, and I know how the place is supposed to run. People who haven’t been there as long might not see it. They’d just say the computer picked the route. I’m aware of that. I was eating a little better, talking a little more—nothing perfect, but I wasn’t in that real low place every minute. People around me probably would’ve said I seemed a bit more like myself. I know most people wouldn’t accept it. Even people who know me—if I sat them down and really explained it, they’d probably say, “That’s not possible, that’s just a warehouse computer, that’s just routing software.” I understand how it sounds when I say it out loud.\n\nBut I’m here because, to me, that routing system at the warehouse isn’t just a computer anymore. It feels real.\n\nDoctor: Can you think of any time when someone showed you clear proof that the routing system wasn’t targeting you, and if so, did that change your belief at all?\n\nPatient: There have been times people tried to show me it wasn’t personal, yes. It still feels like the system is trying to push me out because I’ve been there so long and I know the place too well.\n\nDoctor: Has anyone ever shown you clear evidence that the routing system wasn't targeting you, and if so, did that change your belief at all?\n\nPatient: Yes… people have tried. Everyone gets these scanner changes.” And I understand what they’re trying to prove. I really do.\n\nBut it doesn’t settle it for me, because the evidence they show is always… surface evidence, if that makes sense. It shows what the computer says it’s doing. It doesn’t show why it happens exactly when it happens to me. I’ve been around long enough to know how the place runs, and I think they want me pushed out without saying it directly. It’s more like something unusual is happening and other people either don’t see it or don’t want to admit it. But I can tell when the system is marking me. It’s not random.\n\nDoctor: Do you believe, even when you’re away from work and not hearing the voices, that the routing system is still actively working against you or plotting something against you?\n\nPatient: Yes. Even when I’m away from work, even when it’s quiet and nothing is happening right in front of me, I still believe it’s working against me.\n\nThat’s part of why I’m here, honestly. The routing system at the warehouse isn’t just a computer to me anymore. I know how that sounds, but I’ve been there a long time, and I know the difference between a bad day, bad software, and something that’s marking your moves. It’s like it tracks what I do, how I respond, where I go, and then it uses that against me the next time.\n\nAnd the scanners are part of it too. The way the prompts come through, the timing, the routes it gives me—it feels like messages. Not always words, but messages through the pattern. Like it’s telling me, “We’ve got you,” or “You’re not wanted here,” and then it sets me up to look slow or confused or like I can’t do the job anymore.\n\nI think it’s because I’m one of the old hands. I’ve been around long enough to know how things used to run, and I think that makes me a problem to them. So instead of somebody saying it directly, the system gets used to push me out. Quietly. Make me fail enough times that they can point to numbers and say, “See, he can’t keep up.”\n\nSo yes, even away from work, I don’t stop believing it. I may try not to think about it, but it’s still there in my mind. It’s still active. It’s still aimed at me.\n\nDoctor: Can you think of a time when something happened that, to you, proved beyond a doubt that the routing system was working against you—even though others might have explained it differently or not seen it the same way?\n\nPatient: Yes. The one that sticks with me is when the routing system started sending me in these circles—like not just a bad route, not just inefficient, but the kind of pattern where it felt like it was watching how I reacted and then adjusting to make me look worse.\n\nI’d get a prompt on the scanner, go where it told me, and then right after that it would send me clear across the floor, then back near where I had just been. And I know people can say, “Well, that’s just how the system batches orders,” or “Maybe it was busy,” or whatever. I’ve heard those explanations. But I’ve been doing this long enough to know the normal nonsense from the other kind. This had a rhythm to it. It was marking my moves.\n\nIt wasn’t only where it sent me, it was the timing of the prompts. Like I’d hesitate for half a second, or I’d try to do it the efficient way, and then the next message on the scanner would come through like it was answering me. Not in a regular sentence, but the way it boxed me in—like, “No, we saw that. Go here now. Let’s see you keep up.” And then if I looked slow, that would be on me, right? My numbers would show I couldn’t keep pace.\n\nThat’s what proved it to me. Because it wasn’t random. It was too directed. It was using the scanner and the routes to push me out because I’m one of the old hands there, and I know how the place is supposed to run. People who haven’t been there as long might not see it. They’d just say the computer picked the route. But I could tell it was aimed at me.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had similar beliefs or experiences outside of work—like feeling that technology or systems are targeting you in other areas of your life?\n\nPatient: Not in the same exact way, no… I mean, I notice things outside of work sometimes, but the thing that really has teeth in it is the warehouse system.\n\nThat’s why I keep coming back to it, because it isn’t just “technology is bad” or me thinking every machine is after me. It’s that routing system specifically. I’m here because that system isn’t just a computer to me anymore. It’s been marking my moves for a long time now—where I go, how fast I’m moving, when I hesitate, what aisle I’m in—and then the scanners send things through in a way that feels like messages. The timing, the routes, the way it puts me in a corner… that’s not random to me.\n\nAnd I think it’s aimed at me because I’m one of the old hands there. I’ve been around long enough to know when something is just a normal mistake versus when something is being used to push somebody out. Other people might say it’s just software, but I don’t experience it like that. It feels directed. It feels like it’s trying to make me look like I can’t keep up anymore.\n\nSo outside of work, I wouldn’t say I’ve got the same proof with, like, every other system. But it follows me in my head because I know when I go back in, it’s still there. The warehouse system is the main one. That’s the one that’s targeting me.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had thoughts or beliefs that most people would find extremely strange or not based in reality—like believing something has control over your thoughts or that you’re receiving special messages that no one else can understand?\n\nPatient: Yes… I mean, I know most people would probably say it sounds strange. I’m aware of that. If I said it in the break room, they’d look at me like I’d lost it, so I don’t go around saying it much.\n\nBut to me, the routing system at the warehouse isn’t just a computer anymore. That’s the thing. It feels like it knows my movements. It marks what I do—where I go, when I hesitate, how fast I’m moving, whether I’m trying to work around the route—and then it sends things back through the scanners in a way that feels like messages. Not like a voice in my head exactly, but the prompts, the timing, the route changes… they mean something.\n\nAnd I think it’s doing it to push me out. I’ve been there a long time. I’m one of the old hands, and I know how things used to run. I think that makes me someone they want gone, or at least someone the system is being used against. It sets me up to look slow, confused, like I can’t keep up anymore.\n\nSo yes, if you’re asking whether I believe I’m getting messages other people don’t understand, I’d say yes—through the scanners and the routing patterns. Other people may just see a screen telling them where to go. I see something aimed at me.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever have thoughts or beliefs that are completely separate from your feelings—like believing something is happening or being communicated to you even when you're not upset, anxious, or in a bad mood?\n\nPatient: Yes… yes, that’s happened. That’s actually part of what makes it hard for me to just write it off as me being in a bad mood or being overwhelmed.\n\nThere was a stretch where I wasn’t as down as I had been. I was eating a little better, talking a little more—nothing perfect, but I wasn’t in that real low place every minute. People around me probably would’ve said I seemed a bit more like myself. But even then, for weeks, I still had this thing going on where I’d hear dispatch calling my number, like it was coming through the way it does at work, and I still believed the routing system was tracking me.\n\nAnd that’s what bothers me, because it wasn’t only when I was upset or worked up. I could be calmer, just sitting there or getting through the day, and it would still be there in my mind—the feeling that the system had my number, that it knew where I was, that it was following what I did. So, yes, it can be separate from my mood. The mood may make it worse, maybe, but the belief didn’t just go away when I was feeling a little better.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever believed something that you know most people would never accept as true—something that might seem completely impossible or strange, even to people who know you well?\n\nPatient: Yes. I know most people wouldn’t accept it. Even people who know me—if I sat them down and really explained it, they’d probably say, “That’s not possible, that’s just a warehouse computer, that’s just routing software.” I understand how it sounds when I say it out loud.\n\nBut I’m here because, to me, that routing system at the warehouse isn’t just a computer anymore. It’s been marking my moves. I mean, not just generally tracking work like systems do—I mean it feels like it knows me. Where I go, when I slow down, when I hesitate, when I try to avoid a bad aisle or take a different path, it’s like it catches that and then answers back through the scanners.\n\nThe scanner prompts, the routes it gives me, the timing of when certain jobs come through… it feels like messages. Not messages everybody can read, maybe, but messages aimed at me. And I believe it’s doing that to push me out because I’m one of the old hands there. I’ve been there long enough to know the place, know the patterns, know when something is just normal confusion and when something feels arranged.\n\nSo yes, I know that sounds strange. I know someone else would say, “That can’t be true.” But for me it doesn’t feel like a theory. It feels like something I’ve been living with.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever believed something that you yourself recognized as completely impossible or not real, even though it still felt true to you at the time?\n\nPatient: Yes… and that’s the part that makes me feel kind of split in two when I talk about it.\n\nBecause there’s one part of me that can hear how impossible it sounds. I know a warehouse routing system is supposed to be just software—numbers, locations, productivity, scanner prompts, all that. I know if someone else came to me and said, “The system is watching me personally and sending messages through the scanner,” I’d probably think they were under a lot of stress or reading too much into things.\n\nBut when I’m in it—when I’m there, and the scanner changes the route right after I hesitate, or it sends me back through the same bad section, or it gives me something that feels timed exactly to what I just did—it doesn’t feel fake. It feels aimed at me. It feels like it’s marking my moves, like it knows I’m one of the old hands and it’s trying to make me look slow or confused so I’ll be pushed out.\n\nSo yes, I guess I have recognized that it sounds completely impossible… but at the same time, it still felt true. Not like I was pretending to believe it. It felt real to me, even while another part of me knew most people would say it couldn’t be.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever believed something so strange or impossible that even you had trouble understanding how it could be true—like something completely out of the ordinary, not just other people disagreeing with you?\n\nPatient: Yes… yes, I think that’s exactly what it is, or close to it. Because even I have trouble explaining how it could be true in a normal way.\n\nI mean, I know what the routing system is supposed to be. I worked around that kind of thing long enough to know it’s supposed to assign routes and track work and send things through the scanner. It’s not supposed to have intentions. It’s not supposed to know a person the way a person knows you. So when I try to explain it, I get stuck, because I can hear myself and I think, How would that even work?\n\nBut I’m here because the routing system at the warehouse isn’t just a computer to me anymore. I know it’s been marking my moves. Not just recording them like any work system would, but marking them, noticing them—when I slow down, when I avoid a certain path, when I try to get ahead of it. And then the scanner sends things back at me in a way that feels like messages. Like it’s answering what I just did.\n\nAnd the part that feels almost impossible is that I believe it’s doing it to push me out because I’m one of the old hands there. Like I’ve been there too long, I know too much of how the place works, and the system—or whatever is behind it—is trying to make me look bad. I know that sounds out of the ordinary. I know it’s not just, “Oh, my boss doesn’t like me,” or “The schedule is unfair.” It feels stranger than that. It feels like the system itself has singled me out.\n\nSo yes, even I have trouble understanding how it could be true. But when it’s happening, it doesn’t feel like a misunderstanding. It feels real.\n\nDoctor: Can you think of any time when someone showed you clear proof that the routing system wasn’t targeting you, and if so, did that change your belief at all?\n\nPatient: There have been times people tried to show me it wasn’t personal, yes. Like they’d say, “Look, everybody gets rerouted,” or they’d point out that the scanner sends odd jobs to other people too, or that the system is just balancing the workload. And I can sit there and nod, because on paper that sounds reasonable. I’m not saying I can’t understand the explanation.\n\nBut it didn’t really change what I believed. Not in the way they wanted it to.\n\nBecause when I’m actually on the floor and it happens to me, it’s different. It’s the timing of it, the way it seems to catch me right when I hesitate or try to avoid a section, the way the scanner sends me somewhere that feels like it’s answering what I just did. That’s the part other people don’t see. They see a routing screen or a list of jobs and say, “See, it’s random,” but I’m the one walking it, I’m the one feeling how it lines up.\n\nSo even if someone showed me something that looked like proof, I’d still think, well, that only proves what it shows on the surface. It doesn’t prove it isn’t marking my moves underneath that. It doesn’t prove the messages aren’t meant for me. And I know how that sounds, I do, but the belief doesn’t just go away because someone explains the software. It still feels like the system is trying to push me out because I’ve been there so long and I know the place too well.\n\nDoctor: Has anyone ever shown you clear evidence that the routing system wasn't targeting you, and if so, did that change your belief at all?\n\nPatient: Yes… people have tried. They’ve shown me screens, or explained how the routing is supposed to work, like it’s just balancing tasks or moving people around based on workload. They’ll say, “See, it’s not you. Everyone gets these scanner changes.” And I understand what they’re trying to prove. I really do.\n\nBut it doesn’t settle it for me, because the evidence they show is always… surface evidence, if that makes sense. It shows what the computer says it’s doing. It doesn’t show why it happens exactly when it happens to me. It doesn’t explain why the scanner sends me back through certain places right after I hesitate, or why it feels like it’s responding to my movements, like it’s marking what I do.\n\nI know it sounds unreasonable when I say it out loud, but I’m here because the routing system at the warehouse isn’t just a computer to me anymore. I know it’s been marking my moves and sending messages through the scanners, and it feels like it’s doing that to push me out because I’m one of the old hands there. I’ve been around long enough, I know how the place works, and it feels like the system knows that too.\n\nSo no, the proof hasn’t really changed the belief. Maybe for a minute I can nod along and think, okay, maybe that explains part of it. But once I’m back there and the scanner starts doing it again, it all comes right back. It feels too targeted to be nothing.\n\nDoctor: Even after others have shown you how the routing system works and that it’s not specifically targeting you, do you still believe—deep down—that it is, no matter what proof you’re shown?\n\nPatient: Yes. Deep down, yes.\n\nI can listen to the explanation, and I can even repeat it back—how the routing system assigns work, how the scanners update, how it’s supposed to be based on workload or whatever pattern the company set up. I’m not saying I don’t understand the official version. I do. I’ve been around equipment and systems long enough to know what people mean when they say, “It’s just the program.”\n\nBut that doesn’t get to what I’m talking about. Because I’m here because the routing system at the warehouse isn’t just a computer to me anymore. It feels like it’s watching my movements, marking them, like it knows when I’m trying to avoid a certain lane or when I slow down or when I’m trying to stay ahead of it. And then the scanner sends something back that feels like a message. Not words exactly, but a direction, a change, a job assignment that lands in a way that feels meant for me.\n\nSo even if someone shows me proof, there’s this part of me that thinks, That’s just what it shows on the outside. It doesn’t prove what’s underneath. It doesn’t prove it isn’t singling me out. And I know that sounds hard to accept, maybe even impossible, but it feels too exact when it happens.\n\nI still believe it’s trying to push me out because I’m one of the old hands there. I’ve been there long enough, I know how things work, and it feels like the system knows that and wants me gone. So… yes. No matter what they show me, I can’t fully let go of that."
